| 4.29.62 -> 4.29.63 | 2026-08-05-1153 | OTA | ⚠ THE VOICE STOPS ARRIVING AFTER YOU ALREADY READ IT (twin of golem 1130). Owner: "do we need to see the text and then hear it? that's what makes the voice feel late sometimes, you read it then hear it 10 seconds later." ⚠ THE MECHANISM, AND IT WAS NOT AN ACCIDENT: Kokoro and Qwen share ONE lock (arb159 put them there because running both at once SIGSEGV'd the Tensor G5, and that exclusivity is non-negotiable), and OTA-634 then made the lock priority-aware with its trade written down plainly — "LLM narration jumps ahead of voice synth so the words land promptly and the voice fills in behind". So a line ALREADY ON SCREEN waited behind whatever the model did next, and OTA-1151 measured what that is: a scene_intro generation runs 19.3 seconds. The voice was not merely trailing, it was trailing by the length of the NEXT narration — the reported ten seconds, structural rather than occasional. ⚠ THE TWO SIDES ARE NOT SYMMETRICAL, which is what OTA-634 could not see from where it stood: a narration delayed two seconds is INVISIBLE because nothing is shown until it completes anyway, while a voice delayed ten seconds is the most obvious defect in the game, since you have already read the line it is reading to you. So the order flips and VOICE now outranks the LLM. ⚠ AND A REVERSAL NEEDS AN ANSWER TO THE FEAR IT REVIVES — OTA-634 worried about a voice backlog making responses feel slow, so two things bound it: the total queue cap of three whole lines (OTA-634's own mitigation) and the STALE-LINE DROP added here, which never speaks a line whose text has been on screen longer than six seconds. Past that the audio is an echo of something the player read and moved on from, laid over whatever is happening now, and silence is better; whole LINES are dropped, never half of one, so the backlog cannot grow old. ⚠ THIRD PIECE, PRE-SYNTHESIS: OTA-1152 banked scene intros so the TEXT lands instantly, which made this gap MORE visible — a consequence flagged in that OTA's own handoff rather than left to be discovered — and the bank is also what makes the real fix possible for the first time, because if the line exists before it is needed then so can its audio. A banked line is now synthesised in the same idle window that wrote it, at HOMEWORK priority, and speak() finds the PCM already waiting: text and voice land together, which is the actual answer to the question. ⚠ ONE SHARED CHUNKER, because the cache is keyed per chunk and read at enqueue: a split differing by one character between speak() and presynthesize() would miss every time and miss SILENTLY, so the chunking is one function with one answer. Also recorded, since the owner asked: item-description homework is WRITTEN only and never reaches the spoken channel; a banked scene intro IS spoken; and the welcome-back is five HAND-AUTHORED lines with {name} substituted rather than model output — worth knowing before anyone tries to fix its voice by changing a prompt. New suite ota1153VoiceLandsWithTheText (20 tests); two older suites RE-AUTHORED rather than re-numbered, because OTA-634's headline claim is no longer true while its mechanism is untouched. |
| 4.29.61 -> 4.29.62 | 2026-08-05-1152 | OTA | ⚠ THE SCENE INTRO COMES OFF THE CRITICAL PATH (twin of golem 1129). The owner's call, taken with OTA-1151's arithmetic in front of them: that OTA measured the beat and then said plainly it could not fix it — 19.3s = 3.7 wait + 11.0 read + 3.5 write + ~1.0 other, and even a ZERO-token prompt leaves ~8s. There is no prompt small enough, so the only move left is to stop the player waiting, which is exactly what OTA-1145's bank did for the ambient musing. ⚠ WHY AN INTRO IS PRE-GENERATABLE: same reason a musing is — it is about a PLACE, and the place is knowable before the player gets there. `canonicalLocationAtCell` is a plain index lookup, so the named location on each of the four adjacent cells reads for free, and nothing is built, rolled or mutated to find out where they might step. The CURRENT location is a candidate too and deliberately FIRST: most tiles carry no named location, so a cardinal step usually rebuilds the scene right where the player already stands — the most frequently spent entry, not an afterthought. ⚠ WHAT A PRE-WRITTEN INTRO CANNOT KNOW: weather, hazards, enemies and the vendor are rolled at ARRIVAL, so the prefetch slice carries the destination's STATIC facts only (name, type, authored description) and passes null for the rest rather than guessing — an intro that says nothing about the sky can never contradict the sky. SceneSlice.weather became nullable to say so, which is the type catching up with a `scene.weather?.name` guard deriveEnvironment always had. ⚠ AND THE THREE THINGS A BACKGROUND FILL MUST NOT DO, each a real bug avoided: (1) not MIRROR ITS TOKENS, since partialArbiterText renders live under "The Arbiter:" and streaming a fill would show the player a description of a room they are not standing in; (2) not OWN THE EPOCH, since bumping it would cancel a live narration the player IS waiting on and then cancel the fill itself the moment they act, throwing away the very work the bank exists to keep; (3) RELEASE isGenerating on its own terms, precisely because it never bumped the epoch — the live path's release would not fire and every later generation would wedge behind a finished background job. Everything else is SHARED with the live path on purpose — prompt, streaming, and the whole vetting chain — so the bank stores VETTED prose that has already passed the foreign-word strip, sentence cap, third-person filter, echo detector and off-canon entity guard; a second, quietly different narrator was the thing to avoid. The fill uses the OTHER idle signal: not `uiIdleSince` (stamped by stationary screens, never set while walking, which is exactly when an intro is wanted) but TIME SINCE THE LAST ACTION — six seconds of stillness is reading time, and reading time is when the next room gets written. That lets homework run during normal play, defensible only because OTA-1146 built the harness first: the fill queues BELOW the voice and is cut short the instant a real generation is enqueued. New suite ota1152SceneIntroBank (33 tests); three older suites retargeted, and all three broke the SAME way — an assertion anchored on a text WINDOW rather than on structure. Anchor on the function first, then the block, then on what comes NEXT rather than what encloses. |
| 4.29.60 -> 4.29.61 | 2026-08-05-1151 | OTA | ⚠ THE PROMPT WAS SAYING THE SAME THING FOUR TIMES (twin of golem 1128). Owner: "go after the big ones" — so the big one was PRICED rather than guessed at. The device log gives the exchange rate: `narration:scene_intro ok 19255ms wait 3744ms read 11004ms/write 3544ms in 854t→out 33t HIT-CAP` — 11.0s of reading to produce a 33-token sentence, or 12.9 ms per prompt token. With every line priced, a representative scene_intro measured 3,194 chars / ~888 tokens, of which VOICE_RULES alone was 311 — a THIRD of the whole prompt. ⚠ THE FINDING: three successive OTAs each added a "do not invent places" guard and none removed the previous one, so the model read the SAME RULE FOUR TIMES in four different wordings for ~123 tokens — NO_INVENTED_PLACES, the copy inside VOICE_RULES, the `Location: <biome> - <room>` line, and the bolded `**The player is at "X". If you name any place, it MUST be "X".**`. A 0.5B model given one instruction four ways does not obey it four times as hard; it spends attention reconciling them. The third-person ban was doubled the same way, since SHARED_PREAMBLE bans it ANYWHERE and strictly contains VOICE_RULES' weaker "if a draft sentence BEGINS with". Consolidated to one statement each. ⚠ WHAT WAS NOT CUT: every guard here has a scar behind it — OTA-1054's third-person recap, the "Borderlands" playtest failure, the hallucinated trap sequences — and no guard was removed, only the duplicate STATEMENTS of them. The verb catalog stayed too, because teaching the player the engine's vocabulary through narration is a real feature and the one part a model cannot supply from training data; what went was padding (slash-alternates, the parenthetical item list after "use", six verbs a 20-word aside will never reach for). ⚠ AND THE HONEST ARITHMETIC, the more valuable half: 888 → 760 tokens is ~1.7s off every narration and it is free, but it is ALSO NOT ENOUGH, and the same measurement says why — 19.3s = 3.7 wait + 11.0 read + 3.5 write + ~1.0 other, so even a ZERO-token prompt leaves ~8s because the model writes at 107 ms/token and waits ~3.7s for the native-ML lock. Prompt trimming cannot make scene_intro fast; it has to stop being on the critical path, the way OTA-1145's bank did for ambient. | 4.29.55 -> 4.29.56 | 2026-08-05-1145 | OTA | ⚠ THE BANK (twin of golem 1122). Step two of the LLM headroom track. The economics of an ambient musing were upside down: it is generated ON DEMAND, takes 8-16 seconds, and is then checked against the world it was composed for. The OTA-1130 telemetry says two of three came back unusable — ~16.6 seconds of model time for lines nobody read — and the single biggest killer is `stale`: the player kept playing while the model wrote, so by the time the line existed it no longer belonged to the moment. ⚠ THAT IS A RACE WE WERE NEVER GOING TO WIN BY GENERATING FASTER. So stop racing. A musing is UNPROMPTED by construction (AMBIENT_INSTRUCTION forbids it from reacting to the last action), which is exactly the property that makes it pre-generatable: write them when the world is standing still, spend them when it isn't. TWO SOURCES OF STOCK. (1) REST — the player has explicitly chosen to pass time, the world is standing still by definition, and the shared native-ML lock is as free as it ever gets; fired AFTER the rest resolves so a slow generation never delays recovery, and BEFORE the ambush spawn so a fight leaves the bank untouched rather than poisoning it. (2) ⚠ STALE LINES, WHICH ARE NOT WASTED LINES — `stale` almost always means "you walked somewhere else", and the line is still perfectly good FOR THE PLACE IT WAS WRITTEN ABOUT; banked against its own stamp, walking back into that room spends it instantly instead of paying for it twice. The discard that was the headline waste becomes the stock. SPENDING IS FREE, so the withdraw sits ABOVE the model-readiness and cooldown gates (the bank works while Qwen is reloading, and a line that costs nothing is not rationed by a cooldown that exists to ration generations) and BELOW the combat and tutorial muzzles (which are about whether a musing is wanted at all). ⚠ VALIDITY IS ambientStaleReason UNCHANGED — the same five checks the live path already ran, asked at SPEND time instead of at finish time. That is the whole trick: a banked line cannot go stale between being wanted and being spoken, because there is no gap. Plus the near-duplicate check, which matters more here because a banked line may have sat through a dozen others. Bank is session-scoped, capped at 3, oldest-out, no duplicates; a banked line is DEFERRED work rather than WASTED work so it no longer counts against qwenWasteTotals — that number decides whether a job is worth keeping and must not indict the fix. New suite ota1145TheBank (16 tests); one OTA-1130 assertion retargeted. |

That reading was wrong in a useful way: llama.cpp reuses the longest COMMON PREFIX between the previous prompt and this one, and every prompt we built put a VARIABLE line SECOND (the room name), so the reusable prefix ended after ~14 tokens no matter how much byte-identical text followed. Measured on the ambient prompt: 327 tokens, of which 287 were identical on EVERY call, of which 19 were ever reused — about 2.8 seconds per ambient line at the measured ~10.5ms/token, spent re-reading text the model had already read. FIXED BY ORDER, NOT CONTENT: STABLE PREFIX -> VARIABLE BODY -> IMPERATIVE TAIL. Reusable prefix between consecutive calls, before -> after: ambient->ambient 19 -> 271 tokens; narration->combat 114 -> 456; narration->ambient 14 -> 98. About 2.6s off a repeat ambient line and 3.6s off a combat line. The prompt did NOT get smaller (356 -> 359 tokens on ambient) — this is pure reordering, and the suite asserts it so a later edit cannot "improve" the number by deleting content. ⚠ THE IMPERATIVE STAYS LAST: a 0.5B model follows the instruction nearest the generation point best, so the instruction blocks are SPLIT (rules in front where they cache, task at the back where it binds) rather than moved wholesale. ⚠ THE PREAMBLE IS SHARED ACROSS JOBS ON PURPOSE: the cache holds ONE sequence, so an ambient call after a combat call reuses only what those two prompts share; an identical opening across ambient/peaceful/combat means the preamble survives a job switch. Every sentence keeps its original wording — the only rewrites are the "above"/"below" pointers the move forces (OTA-1131 is the standing lesson that a prompt lying about its own layout costs a whole generation), and combat's "entities listed above" is deliberately NOT flipped because its task still sits after the entity block. That else reads as "the enemy is dead, there is nothing left to counter with" — true in the SOLO fight those paths were first written for, false the moment a pack is involved. | 4.29.52 -> 4.29.53 | 2026-08-05-1142 | OTA | ⚠ THE DORMANCY WINDOW, AND THE WORD THAT HID IT (twin of golem 1119). The owner's device log, three consecutive lines: "OTA session start" / "item_synthesis empty 8809ms read 0ms/write 0ms in 309t->out 0t (0ch)" / "qwen-watchdog: Qwen dormant - reinitializing". 8.8 seconds of wall time with ZERO prefill and ZERO decode — the model did not think slowly, it never ran. ⚠ ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS ORDERING: isDormant() is defined as "status==='ready' but the runtime is gone", and QwenGenerativeEngine.dispose() was PRODUCING that state for the length of its own teardown. LlamaRuntime.dispose() nulls its context SYNCHRONOUSLY on entry (the arb crash fix — detach before release so nothing starts a completion against a context about to be freed), then AWAITS ctx.release() behind the shared native-ML lock, waiting out whatever generation holds it; the engine only set status='idle' AFTER that await returned. From the first line of teardown until the lock cleared, the engine reported ready-over-dead — the watchdog's exact dormancy signature — for as long as the in-flight generation ran (10.4s in the owner's log). The OTA session start is also the one shutdown path that runs in the FOREGROUND, where OTA-1107's don't-reload-while-backgrounded guard does not apply. FIXED by moving one line: status leaves 'ready' first instead of last. Costs nothing, closes the window entirely; the OTA-1107 lifecycleGen bump still comes first of all. ⚠ SECOND HALF: `empty` was hiding two different failures — a model that genuinely produced nothing is a PROMPT problem, a call against a detached context is a LIFECYCLE problem, and the two get investigated in opposite directions. | 4.29.49 -> 4.29.50 | 2026-08-05-1139 | OTA | ⚠ THE ELITE SWAP (twin of golem 1116). The last of the nine difficulty dials (OTA-1136) to find a consumer, and the one the industry survey rates highest: the CONTENT lever. On a hit, the party that would have crested the rise arrives as ONE named body instead. ⚠ WHY THIS LEVER AND NOT ANOTHER MULTIPLIER: a multiplier makes the same fight LONGER — this project has been bitten by that once, and it is why scaledPackSize is welded to scaledSwingCap. A content swap makes the fight DIFFERENT. Four raiders is a targeting and attrition problem; one Reaver-Captain is a puzzle (what is it weak to, do I have that coating, is my resist profile wrong). Those are different verbs — and the second set of systems (bestiary, weakness tags, coatings, resistances) is most valuable against ONE durable body and nearly irrelevant against four disposable ones. It is also structurally incapable of the stall tail: one body is one attacker, so the swing cap can never bind. It is the ANTI-pack, which is why a tier running both hot is more varied than either alone. ⚠ AND IT ADDS NO NEW BALANCE CONSTANTS, which is what makes the claim checkable. scaleEncounterForContext already encodes the rule: a PACK is anchored on one solo-equivalent plus 22% per extra body and gets only 0.6x of the attack/AC bump "because there are several of them"; a SOLO foe gets the full bump. So the elite is the pack's OWN summed HP concentrated into one body, hitting at the solo rate — the fold runs AFTER pack scaling (that total IS the budget), the single body is re-scaled through the solo branch, and the total is given back. Durability from the pack, aggression from the solo; retune the pack and the elite retunes free. ⚠ THE CARRY IS THE ONLY WAY THIS COULD HAVE UNDERPAID: spoils are rolled PER CORPSE, so a body replacing four would have paid one corpse's worth for a four-body fight — paid LESS for a harder encounter, the exact fake-difficulty trap. eliteReplaced buys n-1 extra loot rolls, on the ROLL COUNT rather than a flat grant, so spoils stay drawn from its own table. AUDIT FINDINGS, recorded in eliteSwap.ts: there are NO "kill N of X" objectives in the game (faction quests are fetch / escort / staged, and a staged kill advances ONE stage per kill, so a collapsed party costs encounters, never completions); the +1 max-HP milestone keys on DISTINCT enemy types not total kills (arb119 anti-farm), so collapsing identical grunts costs nothing and a NAMED elite is a new type; hunts match by EXACT NAME, so this must never touch a hunt or boss spawn. Bodies are named for that reason and two more — an unannounced stat-inflated grunt reads as a bug, and the announce lines stop claiming a headcount that is no longer true. New suite ota1139EliteSwap (29 tests). |

The same session's log shows AC drop 16 → 10 mid-fight against five raiders, then a hit on nearly every incoming swing and death four rounds later — "I could have won that five-person fight just by standing there and punching them." ⚠ WHY A PLANNER AND NOT A LOOP OVER equipItem(): slots have CAPACITY. Two chest pieces cannot both go on, a two-hander eats both hands, rings have three slots. A for-loop would "succeed" every call and end with ONE piece worn and four quietly displaced, while the button promised five. A group action's count is a PROMISE, so planGroupEquip resolves contention BEFORE the button is drawn: first-in-list wins the slot, and anything crowded out is NAMED in the bar and again in the confirm. Capacity is seeded FULL rather than from what is worn, because equipping SWAPS exactly as the one-at-a-time path always has; the only thing refused is two SELECTED pieces racing each other, the one case the player could not have predicted. EQUIP and TAKE OFF render BEFORE scrap so the reversible action is under your thumb, and the take-off confirm says what it costs ("your armor class drops by what they were worth") because armor IS armor class and the log above is what it looks like when nobody says so. UNEQUIP clears SLOTS not items — a two-hander holds main AND off, and clearing one would leave it half-wielded. And the take-off confirm says it LOUDER mid-fight ("you are in a fight; every swing coming at you lands easier the moment this is off") because taking armor off in a quiet room is housekeeping and taking it off with five raiders on the tile is the last decision the character makes. New suite ota1137GroupEquip (23 tests). |

| 4.29.0 -> 4.29.1 | 2026-08-03-1090 | OTA | PHASE 5 -- THE ARBITER BECOMES SOMEONE (twin of golem 1067). Plan verbatim: "memory of what you've done, an opinion that shifts with your choices, and an arc across the nine Cores. It has more screen time than any character in the game and currently less personality than any of them." Fair: he was thirteen good lines picked at random, and one of them has promised since OTA-236 "there is a name I have not used in a long time... not yet to you" with no machinery anywhere to keep it -- said on hour two and hour ninety, to a thief and to a hero, in the same tone. engine/arbiterPersona.ts is the machinery, three things kept separate. STANCE (where he is): an arc across the nine Cores -- witness / interested / invested / implicated / named at 0/1/3/6/9 -- rising only, changing WHAT he will talk about. REGARD (what he thinks of you): a score summed from conduct the run already records, banded cold/wary/even/warm/kin, moving both ways, changing HOW he says it via three tones (not five -- five produced pairs nobody could tell apart in play). MEMORY (what he names): the person you wronged, the debt you squared, the Phase 3 question you answered, by NAME; every third remark reaches for one when there is one. ⚠ DERIVED, NOT STORED -- the Phase 3 rule again. Stance and regard are pure functions of the save; the ONLY new persisted field is player.arbiterBeatsSeen, the one-shot lines already SPOKEN, because "has he said this yet" genuinely is not derivable. Absent on an old save reads as "he has not said any of it", so a long-running character hears the beats it has earned rather than silently missing the arc. ⚠ AND NOTHING IN THE MODULE ROLLS DICE: he is one continuous voice, so his opinion must be a function of your conduct, not a coin -- callers pass a rotation counter and the same save always yields the same line. ⚠ THE OPINION CANNOT BE FARMED: every input is clamped and the total clamped again (lore +8, gifts +6, relics preserved +8, forks ±20, wrongs -24, corruption -15, menace -12), so what moves regard late is conduct that COSTS something; the negatives are clamped for the opposite reason, because cold must be a place a fifty-hour run can climb out of. All 29 Phase 3 fork options carry a signed regard value AND an echo phrase in his own words, with a test failing the build if either side has an orphan -- the OTA-1087 audit lesson applied across files. FIVE ROUTES IN, each tested for REACHABILITY rather than for existing: scene narration (the 15% personal-beat branch asks the persona first, old pool only when there is no character to read); arrival (one due beat, yielding to a tide crossing rather than stacking on it -- unspoken means unconsumed, so it lands next time instead of being lost); "what is your name", matched BEFORE every lookup that would swallow it and ALWAYS answered -- the name, "not yet", or "not to you"; the character sheet, with the ITEMISED why, because a hidden opinion score is the Phase 4 legibility failure again; and the ending, where he gives his verdict on you and, at nine Cores with the regard to match, the name he has been holding since OTA-236. The one model touch is a one-sentence brief (stance + tone) appended to the live Qwen persona prompt -- additive, so an empty brief leaves it byte-identical and a failed generation falls through exactly as before. A personality that exists only while a 0.5B model happens to be warm is not a personality; everything else is authored. 55 tests. |
| 4.28.99 -> 4.29.0 | 2026-08-03-1089 | OTA | PHASE 4 -- LET THE DEBT COME DUE, BEHIND A DIFFICULTY TOGGLE (twin of golem 1066). MINOR bump: a new axis of the game, not another patch on the same one. Plan: "the ledger actually calls... you have every substrate already -- hoursElapsed, weather, corruption, faction standing -- none of it currently threatens anything", and in the same breath "⚠ HIGHEST RISK OF MAKING THE GAME WORSE... ship it behind a difficulty toggle and tune from logs". THE TOGGLE (owner's call on the names and the rule): four tiers, Doom-shaped and first person, on a NEW FOURTH STEP of character creation after the motive -- "I only came for the salvage." / "I know what I owe." (DEFAULT) / "Let it come." / "Bury me with them." Each carries a subtitle saying plainly what changes, because a difficulty name that sounds good and explains nothing is a trap on a screen you cannot revisit. ⚠ LOWERABLE MID-RUN AND NEVER RAISABLE: you can always ask the buried country for less, and nobody finishes on "Bury me with them" a run they spent on salvage. It lives on the character sheet afterwards, with higher tiers dimmed rather than tappable so the rule is visible instead of enforced by a refusal. ⚠ WHY EVERYTHING IS NOT ON A DIAL: corruption and weather ALREADY bite (stat penalties, price markup, extra encounters, reposition cost, attack penalty, HP + corruption ticks) and that is shipped, played and balanced -- re-scaling it from a difficulty tier would put a multiplier on top of a year of tuning. So Phase 4 turns the two substrates that threaten NOTHING into pressure and only raises the RATE the other two accumulate: TIDE (time -> scarcity, +4% vendor prices per stage, ~4 in-game days per stage x dial, HARD CAP 6 stages / +24%, and exactly 1.0 for the whole of tier 1); HOSTILE (standing <= -25 and a faction starts FINDING you -- a gate on an interception that was already going to happen, capped at 22%, bounties exempt); CREEP (weather notches corruption faster); EXPOSURE (a storm bites harder). The last two sit AFTER title perks and BEFORE the race immunity at the weather choke point, so earned mitigations still work and a Sentinel is still immune. LEGIBILITY IS HALF THE FEATURE -- the quiet way this phase fails is pressure the player cannot see, so every tide stage crossing gets an authored line once (player.tideStageSeen). Every older save reads as 'owed': the game as it has always played plus the two new systems at their gentlest, with a one-tap way down. 27 tests. |
| 4.28.98 -> 4.28.99 | 2026-08-03-1088 | OTA | PHASE 3 -- MAKE THE STORY ASK QUESTIONS (twin of golem 1065). Build plan verbatim: "the audience that pays premium for text is paying for consequence, and right now you have exactly one fork in the game... extend that shape: 1-2 genuine forks per motive, with lasting consequence. Chapter cards become decisions rather than broadcasts." WHAT MAKES THIS A FORK AND NOT WHAT WE HAD: The Missing's grave/lie/walker resolution is DEALT -- missingResolutionFor() hashes the identity seed -- so it proved the plumbing and the player never chose it. This is that plumbing with the hash replaced by a person. 10 forks, 29 options, 2 per motive, in app/data/story/forks.json. ⚠ FORKS ARE DERIVED, NOT QUEUED, and that is the whole answer to the risk the plan named ("the one place a save-migration bug would be unrecoverable for a player mid-arc"). A queued fork can be lost to a crash, a kill or a bad backfill, and a lost fork is a chapter of the player's story that silently never happens. dueFork(player) is a PURE read of the save: first fork whose motive matches, whose phase gate the run has passed, with no recorded answer. Kill the app in front of the card and it is due again on load; store.pendingFork is a VIEW and is safe to drop at any moment. The only persisted state is the ANSWER (player.storyChoices, forkId -> optionId): absent on every older save, which reads correctly as "asked nothing yet", so there is NO migration to get wrong; an id from a newer build is ignored rather than fatal; and answerFork writes the choice BEFORE paying the effects, so a death between the two costs coin, never a re-asked question. LASTING CONSEQUENCE IN THREE PLACES: (1) now -- an authored line plus coin / a keepsake / one-shot faction standing, keepsakes 'quest'-tagged so a decision cannot be pawned; (2) at the end -- one sentence per answer under WHAT YOU CHOSE on EndingScreen, permanently, beneath the motive epilogue that still closes the arc; (3) in the world -- TopicGate.requiresChoice lets the Phase 2 cast react, three authored (Tellin on the writ, Korash on the districts, Vesryn on the pages sent up early). "Chapter cards become decisions" is done by asking the instant the card is dismissed rather than making the card answerable -- a card holds nothing a fast tap can lose (OTA-1043) and a decision must hold exactly that -- so the fork overlay is the ONE modal in the game with no backdrop dismiss and no close button. Standing here is one-shot BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by a guard, the opposite of the two repeatable acts OTA-1087 had to meter. 25 tests. |
| 4.28.97 -> 4.28.98 | 2026-08-03-1087 | OTA | THE DOORS + AN AUDIT OF PHASES 0-2 (twin of golem 1064). ⚠ MOST OF WHAT OTA-1085 AUTHORED WAS UNREACHABLE and 621 green suites said otherwise -- every Phase 2 test asked whether a topic was AUTHORED and correctly GATED, none asked whether any route in the shipped game reaches it. The TALK chip and the `talk to <name>` router both asked hasTopicsFor(vendor.id), the SPAWN id (roadside_<seed>, overlay_<id>_<ms>), while the topic sets are keyed on the LEDGER id (roadside:grit_maalen) -- wrong namespace, false every time, for all 24 roadside and 5 overlay traders; the 30 named vendors worked only because their raw id happens to equal their ledger id, which is why it hid for three OTAs. `talk to <wanderer>` has always landed on the parley branch, which returns, so all 28 wanderer-archetype topics were dead. An escort leader is not in the scene at all (they live on player.activeFactionQuests) so nothing routed to them. A Core Guardian only ever exists as an ENEMY and every route required an empty scene. FIXED: one talkablePeople() list with one identity function feeding all three consumers; the wanderer conversation is a THIRD option on the parley modal so nothing is displaced (the parley still pays a lead, goods or standing); escort leaders route by name; a Guardian answers questions mid-fight -- but STILL cannot be talked off its post, OTA-806's guardrail is older and outranks this, and the conversation is free precisely because it changes nothing (no damage, heal, turn or enemy counter). All 11 class sets also shipped with the SAME four button labels (a scavenger and a Core Guardian both offered "Ask what they are not saying"); each set now has its own. AUDIT FINDINGS FIXED: (1) ⚠ HOSTILITY WAS A RIVAL-STANDING FARM -- applyRepChange propagates half of any delta to the target faction's RIVALS with the sign flipped, so every standing LOSS is a standing GAIN elsewhere; a refused gift does not consume the item (deliberate, OTA-1083) so -2/+1-per-rival cost only a tap, and beating a vendor into submission was -12/+6-per-rival with the vendor anchored to the room (OTA-1052) so you walk out and back in. Closed by dockHostileStanding: the largest hit already taken for a person is remembered as a MAGNITUDE on their ledger row, a heavier act tops up the difference, a repeat costs nothing -- magnitude rather than a flag is what shuts the downgrade hole. (2) A TOPIC GRANT COULD EVAPORATE: with a lead already in hand the branch printed "the tip will keep" and the caller spent the topic anyway, so a payout gated behind 'familiar' plus a chapter check paid nothing, once, forever; applyTopicGrant now reports whether it delivered and the counter is only bumped when it did, and only when NOTHING landed so a lead+coin topic can never be replayed for the coin. (3) GIFTING REACHED A SMALLER CAST THAN TALKING: escort leaders were talkable and not in the gift picker, which is now derived from the same list; and a gift to somebody with no ledger row consumed the item while writing no memory -- now refused. REPORTED NOT CHANGED: caught theft applies its standing loss on every attempt (same shape as 1, but what repeated stealing should cost is a design call); restitution banks only coin HANDED OVER, so buy-then-resell launders amends at the cost of the spread. 26 tests. |
| 4.28.96 -> 4.28.97 | 2026-08-03-1086 | OTA | THE FLOURISH (twin of golem 1063): the build plan's last unbuilt line and the last item on the owner's seven-item list -- "the LLM contributes at most one short flourish line per exchange, off the critical path, with a template fallback if it's slow". A flourish is not more dialogue: the authored reply owns the words, the flourish owns the HANDS -- one short beat of what somebody is DOING while they answer. 55 authored lines in app/data/npcs/flourishes.json keyed two ways at once, by TRADE (10 buckets, so a smith's hands are not a scholar's; all 30 named vendors map) and by REGARD (6 rungs including wronged, so posture tracks the Phase 1 ledger); pools concatenate and index by a hash of person+topic, so it is deterministic like everything else since OTA-1072. ⚠ THE MODEL IS STILL NOT IN THE CRITICAL PATH. OTA-1081 built the conversation synchronous BECAUSE a 14-20s generation in front of a tapped topic is a loading screen, and nothing about that changed. The order is inverted instead: a request fires when the topic LIST OPENS (the one moment the player is reliably busy reading), the result sits in ONE module slot keyed to that person, and raising a topic TAKES from the slot or takes an authored line and cannot tell the difference. Slow model, dormant model and no model are all the same case -- an empty slot. No await anywhere in the exchange, no spinner, no degraded mode. The slot dies with the conversation and is never persisted. The judge for a generated line is pure and synchronous beside the brief that asks for it: must name the person, one sentence under 160 chars, terminated, no quotes/questions/first-person/second-person-opener (the two registers OTA-1054 caught) and no instruction echo (OTA-1053). Frequency: FIRST raise of a topic only (a re-tread gets the words back but not the business), max 3 per conversation, never the same line twice, silence rather than a repeat once spent. engine/flourish.ts carries the same no-await guard dialogue.ts carries; the test asserts both files plus that emitFlourish contains no await. 30 tests. |

Killing merchants was never designed -- it fell out of the caught-theft path converting the vendor into an ordinary Enemy, which then flowed into the generic defeat routine. It broke contract chains that turn in at that counter (there is no dead-NPC list anywhere, so the death was really a scene wipe the next regeneration undid), and it PAID BETTER than stealing, since the trader-enemy's loot pool is the vendor's own first two offers. A successful theft still hands you the goods; a beating hands you nothing -- they keep hold of the pack, the other stallholders and the watch run you out of a settlement (exitBuilding actually fires, so you cannot re-open the stall tab), and on the open road the trader packs up and goes instead. OTA-808 and OTA-817 later appended members of a different kind (OTA-817 guarantees it) and neither updated the announcer, so a Scrap Drone + Mud Wasp pack read as \"2 Scrap Drones\" and the wasp only revealed itself by swinging. | 4.28.77 -> 4.28.78 | 2026-08-01-1067 | OTA | GOLEM CARD BROUGHT IN LINE (twin of golem 1044): GolemNamingModal carried all three OTA-1066 faults -- instant render over the summon line that states the golem's HP and dice, the cold slate/cyan palette, and no ROLL button at all. Now dwell-gated (2500ms), restyled to the house warm-gold card, and given a 50-name shuffle bag of its own in a deliberately different register from the dog names. |
| 4.28.76 -> 4.28.77 | 2026-08-01-1066 | OTA | DOG CARD: TIMING, PALETTE, NAME POOL (twin of golem 1043): the onboarding card rendered the instant the rescue resolved, covering the fight result; it now holds for any live mission card plus a 4s dwell. Restyled from its cold slate/cyan scheme to the house warm-gold card (MissionCompleteModal reference). ROLL drew from three hardcoded names with replacement -- now 50 authored names from a shuffle bag, no repeat until exhausted. |
| 4.28.75 -> 4.28.76 | 2026-08-01-1065 | OTA | TUTORIAL VOICE RAN A BEAT BEHIND (twin of golem 1042): each beat appends two arbiter lines and the player clears a beat faster than on-device Kokoro synthesises one, so the voice queue grew without bound and the spoken instruction lagged a full beat -- "you'll want a weapon" while already typing `take the rope`. Beat instructions now carry meta.supersede; the controller drops the backlog (keeping the in-air sentence) and fronts the new line. Wires clearQueueKeepCurrent, which had shipped unused. |
| 4.28.74 -> 4.28.75 | 2026-08-01-1064 | OTA | OTA TEARDOWN RUNS CONCURRENTLY (twin of golem 1041): the four native disposes ran in series behind 3s deadlines each -- a 12-second frozen-looking screen, and the leading suspect for the FabricUIManager NPE (player taps a dead screen, touch hits a destroyed surface). The subsystems are independent, so they now race concurrently via Promise.all; worst case ~12s -> ~3s. TTS stops hoisted ahead of the group; deadline timers cleared on win. Also audited all 17 enemy-spawn sites for the OTA-1063 range defect -- no further gaps. |
| 4.28.73 → 4.28.74 | 2026-08-01-1063 | OTA | TUTORIAL CLIMB SOFTLOCK (twin of golem 1040): the summit overlay spawned an enemy during the scripted tutorial climb while the arb108 lockdown still refused every verb but `climb` — the player could not fight, flee, sneak, or use an item, and the refusal never said what was pending. Self-defence verbs now pass whenever enemies are live; each gated beat carries a `remind` the refusal restates; the overlay roll is suppressed for the whole tutorial; the overlay scene sets range explicitly instead of inheriting null. |
| 4.28.72 → 4.28.73 | 2026-07-31-1062 | OTA | AMBIENT FILTER FAILS OPEN (twin of golem 1039): the OTA-1057 instrumentation caught the culprit on the owner's next session — reason=action-opener, on the line "You, my companion, have traveled far and wide…", which is exactly the reflective companion line the feature exists to write. The OTA-1054 whitelist required `you\\s+have` and an appositive put a comma there, so it fell through to "scene narration" and died. The rule is inverted: it now names the BAD opener (present-tense physical action) and lets everything else through. A blocked reflection costs the whole feature; a scene line slipping through costs one sentence. All six prior tests still pass. |

## Catch-up ledger — how 4.1.0 became 4.28.3 (2026-07-26)

MAJOR stayed **4**: same engine lineage since the AI-restructure promotion.
MINOR: 1 (at the freeze) + **27 significant feature waves** shipped between
OTA-603 and OTA-991 = **28**. PATCH: OTAs after the last wave closed (escorts,
OTA-989) → 990, 991, 992 = **3**. The waves, in ship order:

1. OTA-630–631 — instant Crucible forging (background naming)
2. OTA-636–637 — typed-damage combat rework (procs/DOTs, weak/resist gating)
3. OTA-642 — single-active mission system + auto-routing
4. OTA-643–646 — weapon coatings (+ armor resists)
5. OTA-650–655 — fusion picker + crafting-screen overhaul
6. OTA-668–673 — mission tracking + universal contract pause
7. OTA-675/690/707 — throwables & bandolier overhaul
8. OTA-691–692 — faction sigils
9. OTA-710–712 — gesture intent + provokable encounters
10. OTA-716–724/731 — found-only recipe discovery + Rare/Legendary expansion
11. OTA-726–730 — economy/gold-sink wave (paid services, premium stock)
12. OTA-~740–789 — the Hidden Market (square nav, all-faction brokering, rotation)
13. OTA-795 — dodge rework (opposed-contest AC-bypass gamble)
14. OTA-806–809 — social rework: parley + menace
15. OTA-815–819 — engaging-combat rework (scaling, packs, per-spawn weaknesses)
16. OTA-835 — race abilities become real mechanics
17. OTA-836–838 — visible-depth QoL (tap-to-explain, codex/bestiary)
18. OTA-843–845 — emergent depth (Chronicle, World Pulse, The Fallen)
19. OTA-847 — stealth system
20. OTA-849–868 — the LIVING WORLD war arc
21. OTA-869–870 — authored-questline content sprint (all 9 factions arced)
22. OTA-910–915 — the Great Climbs + Skyreacher drop + the Aetherkin
23. OTA-927–930 — the POWER rating + lens slot
24. OTA-947/959 — combat rebalance (defense de-runaway + legibility)
25. OTA-960–967 — loot audit + boss spoils table
26. OTA-973–978 — real-heights climbing arc + reach
27. OTA-985–989 — escort missions
